L1724: Figure 14 displays the comparison of Capmatinib PK across Hepatic Function, divided into two panels.
Panel A: Observed Time-Conc profile across hepatic function
This scatter plot shows the Observed Time-Concentration (Concentration in ng/mL vs. Actual time since first dose in Hrs) profile for three groups: Normal (N=425), Mild HI (N=72), and Moderate HI (N=4). The Normal group shows the highest concentration peaks, reaching up to 15,000 ng/mL. The Mild HI group shows significantly lower concentration peaks, generally below 5,000 ng/mL. The Moderate HI group shows the lowest concentration peaks, generally below 2,000 ng/mL.
Panel B: Comparison of post-hoc CL across hepatic function
This box plot compares the post-hoc Clearance (CL/F in L/hr) across hepatic function groups. The Normal group has the highest median CL/F (around 25 L/hr). The Mild HI group has a median CL/F around 20 L/hr. The Moderate HI group has a median CL/F around 18 L/hr. All groups show similar interquartile ranges (IQR).

The Normal group shows the highest concentration peaks, reaching up to 15,000 ng/mL. The Mild RI group shows significantly lower concentration peaks, generally below 5,000 ng/mL. The Moderate RI group shows the lowest concentration peaks, generally below 2,000 ng/mL.

The Mild RI group has a median CL/F around 20 L/hr. The Moderate RI group has a median CL/F around 18 L/hr. All groups show similar interquartile ranges (IQR).
L1726: Figure 16 displays two plots showing the relationship between Capmatinib Exposure (Quartile) and the Probability of CR/PR (Complete Response/Partial Response).
Exposure-ORR Quartile Analysis Cohort 4
This plot shows the Probability of CR/PR (Y-axis, 0.00 to 1.00) versus Exposure Quartile (X-axis: Q1, Q2, Q3, Q4). The sample sizes are Q1 (N=17), Q2 (N=16), Q3 (N=17), and Q4 (N=17). The plot includes error bars (likely 95% CIs) and a dashed line connecting the means. The probability of CR/PR generally increases from Q1 to Q3, and then slightly decreases or remains stable in Q4.
Exposure-ORR Quartile Analysis Cohort 5
This plot shows the Probability of CR/PR (Y-axis, 0.00 to 1.00) versus Exposure Quartile (X-axis: Q1, Q2). The sample sizes are Q1 (N=13) and Q2 (N=14). The plot includes error bars (likely 95% CIs). The probability of CR/PR is slightly higher in Q2 compared to Q1.
Both plots are based on Time-normalized predicted Cavg.
L1727: Figure 17 displays three time-normalized predicted cumulative incidence (Cav) plots showing the relationship between Capmatinib Exposure Quartiles (Q1, Q2, Q3, Q4) and the probability of adverse events of any grade. The sample sizes (N) for each quartile are Q1 (N=123), Q2 (N=123), Q3 (N=123), and Q4 (N=124).
Exposure-Nausea/vomiting Quartile Analysis: The probability of Nausea/vomiting increases steadily from Q1 (approx. 0.45) to Q4 (approx. 0.65).
Exposure-new G1+ Amylase Quartile Analysis: The probability of new G1+ Amylase increases from Q1 (approx. 0.15) to Q3 (approx. 0.45), then slightly decreases at Q4 (approx. 0.35).
Exposure-new G1+ Lipase Quartile Analysis: The probability of new G1+ Lipase remains relatively stable across all quartiles, ranging from approximately 0.20 to 0.30.

The top right plot (semi-log plot) shows Capmatinib Concentration (ng/mL) versus Time (h) for a single oral dose (0 to 72 h). The bottom right plot (semi-log plot) shows Capmatinib Concentration (ng/mL) versus Time (h) for a 400 mg BID dose (336 to 348 h). In all plots, observed data (symbols with error bars) are compared against PBPK predicted mean (bold lines) and percentiles (dashed grey lines for 5% and 95%, solid grey lines for individual simulated trials).
L1730: Figure 20: Sensitivity analysis of capmatinib Ki value towards CYP2C8. The graph plots AUC Ratio (SCYP2C8 Substrate Repaglinide) on the Y-axis (ranging from 1.0 to 2.4) against CYP2C8 Ki (capmatinib) (μM) on the X-axis (ranging from 0.0 to 10.0). The AUC Ratio decreases sharply as the Ki value increases, starting near 2.2 at 0.0 μM and dropping to around 1.1 at 10.0 μM. A vertical dashed line indicates the In-vitro Ki = 0.9 μM, where the AUC Ratio is approximately 1.4.
L1731: Figure 21: Sensitivity analysis of capmatinib Ki value towards CYP2C9. The graph plots AUC Ratio (CYP2C9 substrate Warfarin) on the Y-axis (ranging from 1.0 to 1.5) against CYP2C9 Ki (Capmatinib) in \mu\text{M} on the X-axis (ranging from 0.0 to 3.5). The curve shows that as the Ki value increases, the AUC ratio decreases, indicating less inhibition. A vertical dashed line marks the In-vitro Ki = 3 \mu\text{M}.
